# Orange Pi RV2 Linux configuration audit

## Scope and conclusion

The vendor X1 configuration is a broad distribution/development kernel, not a minimal RV2 kernel. Its compact seed explicitly sets **1,327** symbols; **1,168 (88.0%)** are under Device Drivers, Networking support, or File systems. The expanded Orange Pi build configuration enables **1,725 built-ins** and **700 modules**.

This audit classifies every explicit `x1_defconfig` entry for the M0 profile and provides machine-searchable catalogs for every emitted full-config symbol and every symbol defined in the branch's Kconfig tree.

### Source pinning

- Kernel branch commit: [`ae9e974d3e19f460b6397bfe8f0f1417a073ce05`](https://github.com/orangepi-xunlong/linux-orangepi/blob/ae9e974d3e19f460b6397bfe8f0f1417a073ce05/Makefile), Linux 6.6.63.
- Kernel seed: [`arch/riscv/configs/x1_defconfig`](https://github.com/orangepi-xunlong/linux-orangepi/blob/ae9e974d3e19f460b6397bfe8f0f1417a073ce05/arch/riscv/configs/x1_defconfig).
- RV2 hardware description: [`x1_orangepi-rv2.dts`](https://github.com/orangepi-xunlong/linux-orangepi/blob/ae9e974d3e19f460b6397bfe8f0f1417a073ce05/arch/riscv/boot/dts/ky/x1_orangepi-rv2.dts) and [`x1.dtsi`](https://github.com/orangepi-xunlong/linux-orangepi/blob/ae9e974d3e19f460b6397bfe8f0f1417a073ce05/arch/riscv/boot/dts/ky/x1.dtsi).
- Orange Pi expanded build config: [`linux-ky-current.config`](https://github.com/orangepi-xunlong/orangepi-build/blob/bdba421984211da19191dc6ac6818a247817335f/external/config/kernel/linux-ky-current.config).
- Kconfig semantics: [Kconfig language](https://docs.kernel.org/kbuild/kconfig-language.html) and [configuration targets/editors](https://docs.kernel.org/kbuild/kconfig.html).
- RISC-V firmware/boot contract: [RISC-V kernel boot requirements](https://docs.kernel.org/arch/riscv/boot.html).

The kernel repository's `x1_defconfig` is the maintainable seed. The Orange Pi build repository stores the fully resolved `.config`. They are related but not identical: the current files differ on nine explicit seed assignments, so experiments must record the exact `.config` actually built.

## What “all menuconfig options” means

- `x1_defconfig`: **1,327 explicit assignments** (517 `y`, 538 `m`, 267 disabled, 5 scalar values).
- Expanded `linux-ky-current.config`: **7,689 emitted symbols** (1725 `y`, 700 `m`, 5160 disabled, 104 scalar values).
- Branch Kconfig universe for `ARCH=riscv`: **16,912 defined symbols**. Only a dependency-selected subset is visible at one time; changing a parent option changes the menu.

Therefore, a static screenshot or a single `.config` cannot literally contain every possible menu entry. The two JSONL catalogs cover both meanings without pretending hidden/unsatisfied symbols are selectable.

## M0 capability profile used for the decisions

M0 boots natively through the existing OpenSBI/U-Boot chain, receives the RV2 DTB, brings all eight harts online, runs a gzip-compressed external initramfs with static BusyBox, mounts proc/sysfs/tmpfs, and exposes an interactive shell on UART0. It excludes Linux-mounted SD/eMMC, Ethernet/Wi-Fi, USB, PCIe, HDMI/display/GPU, audio, camera, input/touch, containers, firewalling, modules, suspend, and production security policy.

U-Boot may read the kernel, DTB, and initramfs from SD/eMMC without Linux having an MMC driver. The MMC stack becomes required only when Linux itself must mount or otherwise access that storage.

## Native boot chain: high-confidence keep set

| Function | Required configuration | Why |
|---|---|---|
| RV2 SoC | `SOC_KY`, `SOC_KY_X1`, `MMU`, RV64/ISA dependency symbols | Selects Ky platform code and the X60 execution model. |
| Eight harts | `SMP`, `NR_CPUS=8`, SBI/HSM support | Starts all eight CPUs; ordered SBI boot is preferred over spin-wait. |
| Device tree | `OF`, `OF_FLATTREE`, `OF_ADDRESS`, `OF_IRQ`, `OF_RESERVED_MEM` | The bootloader passes the DTB in `a1`; Linux uses it for memory and devices. |
| Interrupt/time | `RISCV_INTC`, `SIFIVE_PLIC`, `RISCV_TIMER` | CPU interrupts, external interrupts, and scheduler timekeeping. |
| UART clock/reset | `COMMON_CLK`, `KY_X1_CCU`, `RESET_CONTROLLER`, `RESET_X1_KY` | UART0 explicitly references X1 clock and reset providers. |
| UART pinmux/driver | `PINCTRL`, `PINCTRL_SINGLE`, `TTY`, `SERIAL_PXA`, `SERIAL_PXA_KY_X1`, `SERIAL_PXA_CONSOLE`, serial core | Provides `ttyS0`/console for `ky,pxa-uart`. |
| Early output | `SERIAL_EARLYCON_RISCV_SBI`, `PRINTK` | Matches the DT boot argument `earlycon=sbi`; invaluable during removal. |
| Stock-DTS UART links | `PM`, `PM_GENERIC_DOMAINS_OF`, `KY_PM_DOMAINS`, X1 PDMA and DMA-range support | UART0's DT node references a power domain, DMA channels, and a DMA range. Keep these unless a minimal DTB removes those properties and the driver is verified in PIO mode. |
| Initramfs userspace | `BLK_DEV_INITRD`, `RD_GZIP`, `BINFMT_ELF`, `BINFMT_SCRIPT`, `DEVTMPFS`, proc/sysfs/tmpfs | Unpacks and runs `/init`, supplies device nodes and basic inspection interfaces. |

`RISCV_SBI_V01` is not intrinsically required by modern SBI firmware. Keep it for the first boots, then disable it only after confirming that the board firmware exposes SBI 0.2+ and HSM correctly.

## Device-tree coupling that Kconfig alone cannot solve

The stock RV2 DTB enables many devices—two Ethernet MACs, SD/SDIO/eMMC, USB2/USB3, PCIe, HDMI, GPU, audio, cameras, I2C/PMIC and others. Disabling their drivers while leaving the nodes enabled can cause deferred probes, warnings, and boot-time noise. A defensible minimal kernel therefore needs a paired minimal DTB with unused nodes set to `disabled`.

Two footprint traps are outside ordinary menuconfig:

1. The RV2 DTS requests a **384 MiB** reusable `linux,cma` pool and a 384 KiB DPU reservation. Remove or shrink the CMA node in the minimal DTB after multimedia/DMA tests; merely turning off `CONFIG_CMA` is not sufficient evidence that the RAM is available.
2. The stock boot arguments include `swiotlb=65536`. Linux 6.6 uses 2 KiB SWIOTLB slots, so this requests approximately **128 MiB**, before metadata. Reduce/remove it only after PCIe/USB/high-address DMA are excluded and DMA tests pass.

## Menu-by-menu disposition

| Menu | Explicit entries | Enabled (`y`/`m`) | M0 treatment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Device Drivers | 577 | 328 | Retain only the clock/reset/IRQ/timer/UART/DT chain plus provisional power/thermal dependencies; remove unused device classes and disable their DT nodes. |
| Networking support | 413 | 410 | Disable the whole stack for M0; create a separate M1 network/SSH profile. |
| File systems | 178 | 175 | Keep proc, sysfs and tmpfs; initramfs supplies rootfs. Add ext4/F2FS only for the selected storage profile. |
| Cryptographic API | 47 | 46 | Keep only initramfs decompression and automatically selected primitives unless storage/network/security profiles require more. |
| General setup | 46 | 40 | Keep core init/userspace primitives; remove IPC queues, accounting, cgroups, namespaces, BPF and distribution conveniences. |
| Security options | 16 | 15 | Research M0 can omit optional LSM/integrity frameworks; a deployed edge image needs a separately specified threat model. |
| Kernel hacking | 12 | 8 | Use in equally instrumented observable builds, remove from both release builds. |
| Memory Management options | 11 | 11 | Remove CMA/THP/userfault/page-idle features only with matching DT and workload validation; keep policies constant for fair performance comparisons. |
| CPU Power Management | 10 | 8 | Not required to reach M0; keep thermal/power controls during bring-up, then test a fixed-frequency profile. |
| Library routines | 3 | 3 | Mostly dependency-derived; remove parent features instead of forcing hidden library symbols. |
| Enable loadable module support | 2 | 1 | Disable modules in final M0 and build every critical driver in. Keep modules temporarily during dependency discovery if useful. |
| Kernel features | 2 | 1 | Keep SMP with eight CPUs; do not mix scheduler/preemption changes into the first debloating comparison. |
| Linux/riscv Kernel Configuration | 2 | 2 | Review individually; these are top-level choices. |
| Platform type | 2 | 1 | Keep RV64/MMU and architecture requirements; remove unrelated platform/virtualization choices. |
| SoC selection | 2 | 2 | Keep Ky X1 and silicon errata; AI-core binding is not an M0 boot requirement. |
| Enable the block layer | 1 | 1 | Can be disabled for pure initramfs M0; required for Linux-mounted SD/eMMC or other block storage. |
| Executable file formats | 1 | 1 | Keep ELF and scripts; remove miscellaneous binary format handlers. |
| General architecture-dependent options | 1 | 1 | Mostly dependency-derived; retain what the RISC-V/Ky platform selects. |
| Power management options | 1 | 1 | Suspend/hibernate are outside M0. Be careful: generic PM domains may still be needed by the stock UART DT node. |

## Recommended removal order

1. **Create a recoverable baseline:** preserve the vendor boot entry, serial logs, exact DTB, compiler and full `.config`.
2. **Turn off `COMPILE_TEST`:** it is a build-validation switch, not an RV2 capability.
3. **Remove broad software stacks:** networking, netfilter, Bluetooth/Wi-Fi, extra filesystems, containers/cgroups/namespaces and modules.
4. **Remove unused physical devices:** display/GPU, camera/media, audio, input/touch, USB, PCIe and unused storage; disable the same DT nodes.
5. **Separate observability:** keep perf/tracing/debug in both observable builds only; remove them from both release builds.
6. **Reduce memory reservations:** produce a minimal DTB and command line, then verify CMA/SWIOTLB/runtime memory.
7. **Attempt risky low-level removals last:** PMIC/regulator, CPU idle/frequency, thermal, PM domains and UART DMA only after targeted hardware tests.

Do not begin from `tinyconfig` and guess drivers. Start from the known-working config, remove one dependency cluster at a time, run `olddefconfig`, inspect `scripts/diffconfig`, boot the alternate entry, and execute the M0 acceptance suite after every wave.

## Decision legend

| Decision | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `KEEP_M0` / `KEEP_M0_BUILTIN` | Part of the defined native boot, UART, eight-hart or initramfs chain. |
| `KEEP_DURING_BRINGUP` | Hardware safety or vendor coupling; candidate only after targeted tests. |
| `KEEP_CONSTANT_FOR_EXPERIMENT` | Optional for boot, but changing it would confound performance attribution. |
| `OBSERVABILITY_BUILD_ONLY` | Enable identically in instrumented builds; remove from release builds. |
| `CONDITIONAL_*` | Not M0; belongs to a storage, network or later capability profile. |
| `REMOVE_M0_*` | Not consumed by any M0 acceptance test. |
| `AUTO_DEPENDENCY` | Hidden/derived; change the selecting parent rather than forcing it. |
| `KEEP_DISABLED_*` | Already off and should remain off for M0. |
| `REVIEW_*` | Conservative hold until boot/userspace tests demonstrate it is unnecessary. |

## Catalog field guide

`OrangePi_RV2_Expanded_Config_Catalog.jsonl` has one record for every symbol emitted by Orange Pi's full config. `OrangePi_RV2_All_Menuconfig_Symbols.jsonl` has one record for every symbol defined in the RISC-V Kconfig tree, including hidden dependency symbols and currently invisible choices.

Important fields are `symbol`, `value`, `prompt`, `menu_path`, `help_summary`, `has_menu_prompt`, `visible_under_loaded_config`, `direct_dependencies`, `reverse_dependencies`, `definitions`, `m0_decision`, and `m0_reason`. Visibility is configuration/toolchain-dependent; the source definitions and raw vendor values remain authoritative.

## Limits of this audit

The keep/remove labels are a source-based starting hypothesis for a precisely defined M0 profile, not proof that a generated image boots. Kconfig dependencies, firmware state, vendor-driver assumptions and the DTB must be validated on the physical RV2. A configuration becomes defensibly minimal only after every removal passes repeatable cold boots and the complete capability acceptance suite.
